ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u4	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the Council of Ministers, a draft law on the protection of personal data supplementing and detailing Regulation No 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council, which, to be made clear, will apply directly in the Polish legal order and will apply from 25 May 2018. In particular, the proposal regulates the issues which the EU legislature has left for the decisions of the Member States. The new Personal Data Protection Act will replace the existing... [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Stanisław Tyszka of the Sejm]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u5	Excuse me, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen! I ask you to leave the sitting room if anyone is not interested in what the Minister says. The Members concerned are saying that they do not hear the Minister, so I ask for silence in respect of the office and this House.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u6	Mr. Marshal, I'll try to speak more loudly. The new Personal Data Protection Act, as I said, will replace the existing regulation in this area since 1997. It is obvious that personal data is in today's, especially the digital world, a commodity like any other. We live in times where it is difficult to keep our privacy, and our personal data may fall into the wrong hands and be abused in accordance with our intentions. The Personal Data Protection Act and the EU Regulation are the answers to these realities. Answers in which it was recognised that, as the owners of personal data, we have the full right to have them at our discretion and that the use of such data should be subject to sanctions. The importance of regulation of personal data has a global dimension, because it affects each of us, and technological development makes the opportunities of the virtual world increase as we operate in it. At the same time, we must remember that data, including personal data, is a specific oil of the 21st century and at the same time an opportunity for the development of companies and the entire economy, and access to them is an opportunity to build new, creative businesses. So creating a new law in this area, it is vital to remember About opportunities, but also about threats. How difficult it was to ensure a proper balance between these two extremes, let it be borne out by the fact that the legislative process for creating GDPR was one of the longer ones in the history of the European Union. The creation of the bill was preceded by numerous meetings dedicated to entrepreneurs, non-governmental organisations, representatives of churches and religious unions. or citizens. In the course of the public consultation which was formally ongoing until 13 October 2017, more than 110 organisations and citizens submitted their opinions to the Act, which made a total of 640 comments. The position of the Minister of Digitisation regarding the comments made during the consultation of the draft Personal Data Protection Act was discussed in detail at the conference held in the column room of the Sejm on 15 January 2018. The transparency of the bill-making process was very important. These efforts have been recognised by the social partners. As indicated by the Panoptykon Foundation, the Ministry of Digitalization ensured that the work on the implementation of the GDPR was not carried out under pressure of time, and gave all stakeholders more than 30 days to present their positions. For months, experts from the Ministry of Digitalization have announced the fundamental assumptions of the reform and the planned actions of the government in the media, which has also fostered transparency and social engagement. Since the beginning of our legislative work, our aim has been to improve the enforcement process in Poland of breaches of personal data protection rules by establishing an effective body for the protection of personal data. The proposal provides for the appointment of the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data, which will replace the existing General Data Protection Officer. The President of the Office will be appointed for a four-year term by the Sejm of the Republic of Poland with the consent of the Senate, and in the performance of his duties will be subject only to the Act. The President of the Office will support a larger number of deputies - now there is one, and the law assumes that there will be three of them - and the advisory body for him is to be appointed in the draft Board for Personal Data Protection. The Council will, through its contribution, be a forum for representation of positions of different backgrounds. Similar bodies have been working very successfully in other legal orders for years, e.g. in Austria, supporting data protection authorities. The Council will be composed of eight members and candidates appointed by the President of the Office will be able to submit inter alia. Council of Ministers, chambers of commerce, scientific units and foundations and associations. The Council will be appointed for a two-year term. The project also provides for mechanisms to reduce the length of proceedings due to breaches of personal data protection rules. The provisions contained in the draft prejudge that no more than 30 days are allowed to be checked on breaches of personal data protection rules. We also introduce rules of civil and criminal liability for breach of personal data protection rules. The Penalization will include intentional breaches of ordinary data, sensitive data and obstruction of compliance controls by the data protection authority. The project details the level of financial penalties for public authorities which may be imposed by the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection. Maximum penalty for a public body within the meaning of Article. The 9th Public Finance Act amounts to 100,000 according to the project. PLN, while for cultural institutions - 10,000. zł. The Act introduces new, commercial data solutions. First of all, it is important to mention the certification mechanism, which will confirm that the institution processes personal data correctly, ensuring protection of privacy. The certificate will be issued by the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection or by bodies accredited by the Polish Accreditation Centre. This will have a positive impact on increasing customer confidence in a company that will have such a certificate or citizens' confidence in public authorities. Another option provided for in the Act is the Code of Conduct, which will constitute a set of good practices for the processing of personal data, taking into account the characteristics of the environment concerned. The Codes respond to the difficulties arising from the fact that the EU regulation does not explicitly state how personal data should be protected. The guidelines created by industry representatives, in which processing activities are repeatable and easily identifiable, will provide specific guidelines on privacy protection. The Code drawn up and consulted will have to be submitted to the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection in order to verify its compliance with the GDPR. Once successfully completed, the Code will be a set of binding guidelines for the protection of personal data dedicated to entities operating in a specific environment. The project also requires the President of the Office to issue recommendations dedicated to specific sectors and indicating possible ways of securing personal data in specific sectors. Their immediate edition is certainly counted on Polish entrepreneurs. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Personal Data Protection Act is a compromise between the freedom of economic activity that the government particularly cares about and the need to protect citizens' privacy. When presenting this proposal, I ask the High House to accept it kindly. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u7	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in its discussion on this item it would hear ten-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and a five-minute statement on behalf of the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Edward Sulfur, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u8	Mr. Marshal! Minister! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present our position on the government's draft Personal Data Protection Act, contained in print No 2410. Today's governmental submission concerns the protection of personal data and results from the need to apply from 25 May 2018 Regulation (EU) 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of personal data of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and repealing Directive 95/46 of the European Community. The Regulation is more known as the Personal Data Protection Regulation, i.e. the so-called Regulation. GDPR. It is worth recalling here that the regulation will apply, as the Minister said, in all the countries of the European Union, and will be binding on all those who process personal data. The Regulation will be an act directly applicable in each Member State which is required to effectively apply the provisions of the Regulation in its legal order. The GDPR Regulation therefore harmonises the principles of the protection of personal data in the European Union and thus the application of the law by data protection authorities, so the government has developed a new law on the protection of personal data which will replace the 1997 Personal Data Protection Act. A new competent authority for the protection of personal data, to which the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data, will be established. The President of the Office and his office will be the successor of the General Data Protection Officer. The President of the Office of Personal Data will appoint the Sejm for the term of office to ensure full independence of the President during this period. The body assisting the President of the Office shall be the Board for Personal Data Protection. Detailed information on the competence of this board has been included in the draft law. I'm not going to present them in detail here. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The new provisions on the protection of personal data clearly state that personal data are information that allows the identification of a natural person. This may be information, starting with your name, PESEL number, IP number, location data or data that reveals the history of our favorite purchases. All information collected about a person that allows him to be identified is therefore personal data. Therefore, the Act under discussion today applies to the natural persons concerned in relation to the processing of these data. I would point out that the law does not apply to the protection of legal persons concerned. The GDPR Regulation lays down the rules for the processing of personal data which affect the data subject. Data processors are institutions that process them on a large scale. These are both public entities and data-processing operators, different analytical institutions which, due to the nature or purpose of the activity, require monitoring. All these entities must have a designated personal data protection officer. Each inspector will be notified in the light of the new rules. However, the Act does not specify the specific qualifications that the Data Controller should have. The choice of the inspector, and therefore his skills, will always be the responsibility of the administrator, in whose interest it is for the inspector to carry out his tasks properly. It will be up to the new inspectors and administrators to comply with the rules and procedures for obtaining consent for the processing of personal data. It is worth recalling that, in the light of the GDPR regulation, consent to the processing of our personal data must be voluntary, specific, conscious. This means that consent forms must be written in a clear language and in a clear way. Any natural person who consents to the processing of his or her data is to be aware that he or she has given such consent. If she does not have such awareness, then actually such consent will be expressed improperly. The consent to the processing of data will have to be properly recorded by each controller and will be subject to detailed checks. Anyone who consents to the processing of data may withdraw it at the appropriate time. It's important that we're aware of that. The GDPR Regulation also protects children under the age of 13 whose data can be processed with parental consent. The Regulation confers on individuals the right to have access to the information concerned, the right to request the rectification and completion of personal data, the right to object to the processing of data, the right to transfer data and the right to be forgotten. These are issues which are very clearly highlighted in this regulation. All these rights of natural persons will be subject to the control of the President of the Office of Personal Data. Violation of the provisions of the Personal Data Protection Act will be prosecuted under Polish law in accordance with administrative proceedings by the President of the Office. Decisions will be issued by the President of the Office as a competent authority in matters of personal data protection and will be brought before the administrative court. This draft law lays down the principle that fundamental sanctions for breaching personal data protection rules will be imposed on the controller or the processor of the financial penalties concerned. The amount of these penalties was also presented by the Minister in his speech. On the other hand, criminal penalties are foreseen for obstructing controls by authorities which are entitled to monitor the application of the data protection rules. In this draft law, the government proposed that all persons who act as an information security administrator under the current law should have until 1 September 2018 to register as a personal data controller. All persons who are today responsible for data security must make an informed decision as to whether they wish to act as a personal data controller and submit a notification to the President of the Office. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! In view of the importance of the draft Personal Data Protection Act, the Law and Justice Club recommends further investigation of this project in the relevant committees. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u9	Thank you, sir. Please take the floor of Mr Arkadiusz Carrot from the Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u10	Dear Marshal, Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The bill we are discussing is one of the most important acts adopted by the Sejm of the current term of office. GDPR is a word that has been changed thousands of times in recent months by all cases, by all those who process personal data. The aim of the project is to adapt to Polish law the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data. Each Member State shall ensure its effective application in its legal order by adopting the relevant internal rules. The regulation will begin to be an act directly applicable and directly effective in several days. These provisions shall be addressed to all entities which collect or use personal data of natural persons. Since 25 May 2018, all companies, offices, schools, NGOs and not only will have to comply with these requirements. In today's world, every piece of information has its price, while personal data has become the currency of the 21st century for many and are at the weight of gold. For this they are often used without knowledge and against the will of those who own this data. Therefore, the regulation of the EU institutions is intended to give citizens a guarantee of security and a fresh look at their privacy issues. The Regulation applies to practically every data processor and guarantees citizens an important right to privacy. This includes, first, the right to be forgotten, i.e. the right to permanently delete personal data processed by the entity, second, the right to request the transfer of data, i.e. the possibility to transfer personal data to another entity when changing the contract, thirdly, the extended right to information on what data, for which purpose and by whom data are processed, fourthly, the right to access personal data of the data subject and fifthly the extended right to object to the processing of data. These are important tools that guarantee citizens the security of their data and a sense of privacy. The process of implementing EU legislation and preparing a new draft law on the protection of personal data took many months. During the work on the law, fortunately, many changes were made, which were recommended by the social side during the public consultation. For example, initially small and medium-sized enterprises could hope that GDPR would not concern them. The Ministry of Development wanted to exclude them from the information obligation. This would mean that their customers and as much as 99% Polish companies employ less than 250 people, they would cease to be informed about what is happening to their data. However, these records were withdrawn by the Ministry from the draft of this Act. The General Data Protection Officer carried out a study which shows that only 8% more than two months ago entrepreneurs have started the implementation of the General Data Protection Regulation in their enterprise. Entrepreneurs pointed out that there was no basic aid from public authorities to comply with the new rules. In their view, in particular, the shortcomings concern, firstly, broader information for small companies which do not know to what extent the General Data Protection Regulation applies to them, secondly, education on new obligations, thirdly, to discuss the changes in the General Data Protection Regulation of profiling rules and risk-based approaches and accountability, fourthly, guidelines on new obligations explained on specific examples, fifthly communication on the General Data Protection Regulation on the Internet. The last such doubts concern the lack of online training, in particular in the form of e-learning. The Ministry of Digitalisation has prepared only an electronic guide, which is essentially the only form of support for entrepreneurs in implementing the provisions of the General Data Protection Regulation. However, it is mostly an interpretation of the provisions of the Regulation, which is not a step-by-step guide to how the trader is to behave in a new situation. Under these circumstances, government action should be directed towards explaining how to carry out the process of change rather than translating the content of individual rules. What the Ministry has done in this area is far from enough. In this respect, there is considerable doubt about the resolution of the authority responsible for supervising the compliance with the data protection rules, which is the General Data Protection Officer, and replacing it with the new Personal Data Protection Office. None of the provisions of the Regulation require a change to the authority responsible for supervising these data. As experience has shown so far, the current model of GIODO's status and independence guarantees provides very high standards and meets the requirements of the Regulation for supervisory authorities. Furthermore, GIODO has become a recognizable body dealing with these issues. Over 95% Entrepreneurs shall identify this entity as being responsible for the protection of personal data. It's more than 20 years of experience that the Ministry wants to destroy. Therefore, it is worth asking about the costs of such a change - both financial, legal and social - regarding the recognition of GIODO in the awareness of entrepreneurs, public administration and above all citizens. It seems unreasonable to bear such costs where a change in the name of the authority is not required by the Regulation. Fortunately, the provisions concerning the appointment of the Deputy President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data by the Prime Minister were withdrawn from the protection of the independence of the personal data protection authority from the point of view of guaranteeing the independence of the personal data protection authority, which would indicate an attempt to subordinate such an important function to the executive authority. And one last thing. Today, around 150 people work at GIODO. Additional money is necessary for this institution to hire another 100 people and create new jobs. This is the minimum that will allow to carry out so far numerous tasks resulting from the GDPR. Even if the budget is frozen in May because it is currently in the budget reserve, it will be too late. For how are the reports of personal data breaches to be accepted, including data leaks, in what program, on what equipment, who will receive and consider these reports? After all, it takes time to prepare tenders, buy equipment, servers, or hire additional people. And these are the doubts we're paying attention to.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u13	Thank you very much. Please take the floor of Mr Bartosz Józwiak, Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u14	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The Government's draft Personal Data Protection Act aims to implement EU law or to adapt Polish legislation to the applicable EU regulations on GDPR, which in any case will become applicable in Poland from 25 May 2018. The Act shall apply to the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data within the scope laid down in Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and on the repeal of the previous Directive. This law sets out a number of aspects that relate to the protection of personal data, including the protection of personal data. specify the public entities required to appoint a personal data protection officer and the procedure for notification of his designation, the conditions and procedures for accreditation of the certification body, the monitoring body of the code of conduct, the certification procedure, the procedure for the approval of the code of conduct, the competent authority on the protection of personal data, the infringement procedure for the protection of personal data, the European administrative cooperation procedure, the control of compliance with the rules on the protection of personal data or civil liability for the breach of those rules and the proceedings before the court. The Act will also regulate the procedure for notification of personal data protection inspectors and matters relating to entities obliged in the Polish legal order to appoint this controller, as well as the issue of the creation of a new national system for the protection of personal data, headed by a supervisory authority, which according to the designer should be the President of the Office for the Protection of Personal Data. This is a new supervisory authority which, in terms of its competence, including, for example, to impose administrative financial penalties, will be significantly different from the current office, i.e. the General Data Protection Officer. The Act regulates the procedure for dealing with breaches of data protection rules. This is mainly aimed at preventing the ongoing chronicity of these proceedings by abolishing the administrative duality in order to obtain judicial protection more quickly. Here, the organ has the possibility. A provision is also introduced in the Act, in the light of which the investigation of data breach cases may not last more than one month. In addition, according to the Act, the President is required to issue recommendations addressed to entrepreneurs regarding the principle of the protection of personal data. The draft regulates civil liability issues for infringements of these provisions, which is important, reduces the penalty resulting from the Regulation. The President of the Office for Personal Data Protection will also be able to request a legislative initiative in matters relating to the protection of personal data. These are just some of the elements that this project introduces. The project, which actually in some sense also alleviates the elements that the GDPR introduces, which would still apply in Poland, whether we implement this law or not. And really personal data and their protection is one of the basic elements today. In a cipherized society, in which personal data is, to a large extent, a commodity of the highest importance or a commodity that is very desirable, this is of great importance. We need to focus very much on this, especially since in many other situations we are opening up new access to this personal data. We give them to the next service, the next institution. We are expanding our capacity to collect personal data about the citizens of Poland, citizens of the Union, and therefore it is necessary to introduce maximum security systems for the protection of personal data, systems that will not allow us to abuse those rights, which we give to very different services, because there are no specific restrictions in the laws on specific services. There are very few safety valves that protect us against our personal data being used inappropriately or not over-used by individual services and institutions. This also applies to the trading of data within the functioning of companies or to the general market. However, there are two fundamental problems with this law. First of all, this is what has already been mentioned here. In fact, we had two years to deal with this law, and in fact there was probably a very serious consultation, work was done on this law, but at the end the most important institution, which is the Parliament of the Republic of Poland, receives in principle 2.5 days for this law to be carried out by the Sejm. For 2.5 days we are to bend over a very serious law concerning all Poles in a huge and very sensitive range. That's practically impossible to do. We get this bill, very serious and big, in the middle of May weekend, suddenly we're going to take care of it and do it again, push it in the knee. Ladies and gentlemen! These are not the rules of good legislation, because we take responsibility for something that we are not fully able to handle and work honestly. This is something that it would really be worth thinking about, that we, parliamentarians, should not put ourselves in this position, because then we are accused of being a bad law. How are we supposed to make a good law in two and a half days with such a serious bill? It's a huge problem, and it's not the first law that's being processed like this. Yesterday we had a bill on the transfer of passenger data on air travel - exactly the same. In a few days, this law must enter into force because this is what EU law requires. And again, we have three days to consider and adopt a very serious law that extends access to sensitive personal data. This isn't right. Moreover, in that project we use the name of the data protection officer's general institution at many points, so we will be updating that law in a moment. In my opinion, the order is also disturbed here and this is not how it should be. If we have such an important law, perhaps it would be worthwhile to first proceed over this and then over the second one, which refers to the new formula of the organisation of the entity responsible for the protection of personal data. So it's a matter of reflection, Minister. It's really not the opposition's malice, but maybe we should do things according to a certain schedule, because I can't imagine how the Senate is going to lean on it. He's gonna do it again by pushing his knee. In this way, we will not be able to make a good law. Of course, we as a Kukiz club15 understand the need for these rules, so we are in favour of directing this to further proceding. On the other hand, it is absolutely difficult for us to agree with such a formula, with such a procedure and with such action. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It was also not decided to restrict the application of personal data protection rules only to the processing of data in connection with the activities carried out by the administrative unit of the controller or the processor in Poland. The government has decided in this proposal to introduce provisions defining the scope of the provisions on the protection of personal data for the national security of the State. The draft law also regulates the procedure for notifying personal data protection inspectors and entities required in the Polish legal order to appoint a personal data protection officer. The draft law proposes that the obligation to appoint a Data Protection Officer should apply only to the catalogue of cases described in the Regulation. It has not been decided to extend those concerned in the situation of compulsory appointment of an inspector arising directly from the provisions of the Regulation. High Izbo! The draft law also describes the rules of certification and the procedure. It was proposed that in the Polish legal system the supervisory authority, which we know will be the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection and the certification bodies whose competence will be determined by the President of the Office. The certification shall be carried out at the request of the controller or processor, the manufacturer or the entity placing the service or product on the market. The certification criteria, as I said earlier, will be defined by the President of the Office, but in this case the Board for Personal Data Protection will have a role. The Act also proposes to regulate the amount of the fee charged for certification activities by the President of the Office. High Seym! Regulation 2016/679 lays down, in principle, the need for Member States to establish a new national system for the protection of personal data. According to this project, the supervisory authority will be the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection. In accordance with the Regulation, it is also intended to be an element of the protection of the personal data in relation to the processing of tasks and the exercise of other powers in the manner described, completely independent. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u46	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! This project will allow for the gradual start-up of individual functionalities as the work progresses and appropriate testing of them, thereby determining the willingness of the relevant stakeholder group. Such rules will eliminate the risks associated with the simultaneous activation of all system functionalities and integration and communication including more than 60 stakeholder groups. At the same time, this draft law introduces provisions on the exploitation of the potential of central vehicle registration. It is proposed to exempt citizens moving on the territory of the Republic of Poland by vehicles registered in Poland from the obligation to carry with them a document of entry into service, i.e. registration or temporary permit, as well as the need for a paper OC policy. At the same time, road traffic control authorities will be able to verify the vehicle data, including its documents, in the central vehicle register without the need for drivers to present these documents. The project also adapts the provisions on retention of registration or temporary authorisation, introducing provisions that allow the so-called. virtual retention of this type of document, which will be recorded in the system each time. At the same time, provisions were added on the transmission of information about the dismantling of vehicles directly from the dismantling station to the central vehicle register, and therefore the collection of data about dismantling stations, which will be provided by the Marshal of the voivodship. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you very much.

Also, by dispelling your doubts about the second thread, whether the problem regarding the implementation of the CepiK IT system - and probably in the guesswork of everyone else - is related to the issue of remuneration in the public sphere or at the Central Information Technology Centre, I can say that this is certainly some aspect, especially in the context of supervisory staff working in ministries and government agencies, while as far as the Central Information Technology Centre is concerned, by building this employment pyramid accordingly, we are able to get closer to a more or less market level, so that at this moment the saying... This is not an alibi that I would like to use right now, saying that there is no computer scientist who would like to work at COI, because in general, as far as this perspective is concerned, we can provide you with a relatively attractive remuneration. These are not top forks, but more or less the average wage, which is valid on the market, which is very demanding. Generally, all companies in the IT industry have a problem with getting employees, but it's as if COI is not in a lost position here. COI, this knowledge just doesn't stay in the organization later. With regard to Mr Carrot's question, I can answer this: as regards progress in the area of central driver records, we cannot and do not want to hide that due to the problems that arose after the implementation of this vehicle-related part, most of the efforts of the organisation, including the CEPiK team, were directed towards the time of system stabilisation and repair of errors, possibly implementing urgent changes and extensions, which were thus diagnosed by the elderly or by the citizens, so this degree of progress, among others. for this reason, it did not increase significantly, but above all, which is probably more important, we, too, when recognizing some problems at the implementation stage of the CEP, did not want to duplicate the same errors too hastyly and we considered that in this first instance it was important to think again about the construction itself, the architecture of the system, how the central system works with these support applications, especially with the local applications SI Vehicle and SI Driver in the case of the second part, because experience has shown that this contact, this synchronization and the combination of these two systems are simply critical. As far as messages are concerned, they do not have dates without kosers, precisely because these dates will be based - the first approximation of them at this point - on a timetable that is in preparation and, as experience shows, unfortunately at the last stage there may be some events, new circumstances, new errors that will result in that ultimately for the security of implementation we will want to move these deadlines towards the schedule originally planned. Responding to certain concerns or accusations - which I do not directly answer - that this is a delay in implementation on the holy one, I want to assure you that, among other things. This change, which is introduced in this Act, i.e. the possibility of getting rid of the need to carry documents, results, among others. from the fact that we've just implemented a central vehicle register, otherwise it wouldn't be possible. We also see further changes and topics in which we can make life easier for citizens, drivers - whether by dealing with the issue of driving licences or by giving the possibility of not driving a driver's licence. We feel that we will clearly be accountable for how this system is implemented, while the experience of this first stage shows that working and acting taking into account a specific, rigid date unfortunately puts on a straitjacket, which at some point just starts to decide a little bit whether to influence the decisions taken. In my opinion, ultimately, this could cause more damage than benefits. As far as these subsequent communications are concerned, I am sure that within the steering committee that works, it is established for this project, we will signal in the communications within the project schedule... The members of the Steering Committee shall be representatives not only of the departments but also of other stakeholders, including local authorities. This project has gone through exactly this way, especially with the ancients, with the departments of communication, with the associations that participate in these works, as well as with the Ministry of Infrastructure, which is our main partner, or with the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. So, let's say the claim that the project is unprepared or specially sent to the Sejm at the last moment is certainly false. You can expect an initiative from us in a short time. As regards the current situation in the CEPiK system, I would like to assure you that this stabilisation period, which lasted longer than we expected, can be said to have come to an end in practice and can be measured, for example. the number of notifications that are recorded in the helpdesk system in the ancients. The level of notifications before 13 November - before implementation, it was about 200 applications per day and for a few weeks this level of applications has been maintained, so it can be said that we have returned to normal use of the system. As far as statistics are concerned, the number of documents and time permits processed are at virtually the same level as before implementation, or even higher. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u70	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I present the position of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle regarding the government draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and some other laws. The purpose of this Act is to prepare improvements to the CEPiK 2.0 system . In addition, the proposal provides that the existing provisions apply until technical solutions are implemented to enable implementation, transmission and collection. One of the changes that is most interested in drivers is the exemption of citizens moving vehicles registered in Poland on the territory of the Republic of Poland from the obligation to carry with them a registration document and a document confirming the conclusion of a mandatory civil liability insurance contract of the vehicle holder. The possession of these documents will not be required in the case of road inspections. The application of the provision is conditional on the implementation of technical solutions which will, in principle, provide access to the verification of the data of these documents. On-line at the CEP. This change will bring great comfort to citizens, which should be commended. The amendments will be governed by Article. 38 laws. The consequences of this modification are changes to the provisions concerning the tasks of road traffic control authorities when verifying the vehicle concerned. The approach to retention of registrations, time permits will also be modified. Where there are grounds for retention of these documents, a solution was adopted to refrain from physical retention by road traffic control authorities in order to mark the detention of that document in the Central Vehicle Registration. The aim of the CepiK 2.0 IT programme is the reconstruction and modernisation of the CEP, CEK, as well as the construction of the CEPKP. Rules for clarifying non-compliance will be laid down. The aim is to create a uniform technical framework for the entry of data into the database, to lay down the rules for the entry of data in the records at the time of carrying out the activities resulting in the transmission of data and to extend the directory of entities authorised to access data stored in the records. In view of the changes made and the acquisition of competence in the conduct of the CEPiK 2.0 project and the supervision of the unit responsible for the establishment of the CEPiK 2.0 central base, the inventory of the current status of the project, the date of entry into force of these provisions of 1 January 2017 was postponed to 4 June 2018. In addition, new structures have been set up to manage the project. Due to the transfer of a large number of data from multiple sources and the switching of a large number of entities, the Ministry decided that the CEPiK 2.0 programme would be based on the phase-in implementation of the project and not, as originally established, the system would be launched at one time. The changes that will be made will be on a large scale. Suffice it to say that there will be 380 counties involved, over 4,000. vehicle control stations and more than 7 thousand. Driver training centres. The implementation of the system is therefore divided into six stages. This project also changes the scope of data collected in the register of dismantling stations and vehicle collection points - this is governed by Article. Point 4 (1) (a) b Act. The current legal situation requires that information on dismantling be provided by the governor on the basis of a certificate sent by the dismantling station or presented by the owner. This obligation will be transferred to the source entity. This approach - by eliminating old-age mediation information in the flow - provides faster access to current data for system users. Due to the awareness of the changes to be made to ensure that the CEPiK 2.0 system works properly and to satisfy citizens, but at the same time it is transparent and functional for service providers, the Free and Solidarni Circle supports the proposed changes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u141	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I took part in the presentation of a report by the Committee on Social Policy and the Family on the draft law on special solutions supporting people with significant disabilities, print No 2479, and the report by the committee is print No 2489. April 30th this year Ww. The project entered the Sejm. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on 7 May 2018. The contested bill introduces solutions that aim to cover people with a significant degree of disability regardless of their age with specific forms of support. It will bring real savings in the household budgets of those families to households where there is a person with disabilities. This is another step towards a systemic solution to the difficult and complex situation of people with disabilities and their families. The Law and Justice Government has consistently implemented programmes dedicated to people with disabilities since its oath. Only in 2016 and 2017, spending on carers and people with disabilities has increased to almost 3 billion. We know that this is still not enough, but in addition to financial expenditures we need to rebuild a fragmented, inconsistent system of case law, support and assistance for people with disabilities. The proposed draft law proposes the abolition of the periods of use of medical devices set out in the Refund Act for medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices. The right to benefit outside the order of health care services and pharmaceutical services provided in pharmacies. Once the proposed law has entered into force, people with a significant degree of disability will have the right to benefit from specialized benefits without having to receive a referral. The revised bill provides for the abolition of the financing limit for health care services in the context of contracts concluded by the National Health Fund and financed by its budget. The proposed regulations will result in the spending of funds from the National Health Fund's financial plan. The project is not covered by European Union law. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy at its meeting yesterday following its first reading, discussion, consideration and submission of two amendments, one of which was editorial and legislative, and this amendment was positively adopted... A second amendment was also tabled, which was negative by the committee. It was reported as a minority request. This was Mrs Agnieszka's amendment to the Prosecution and concerned payment to the recipient having a decision on a significant disability of the amount of the aid allowance of PLN 500 per month. After considering the project as a whole with 20 votes in favour, 14 abstentions and no votes against, the committee welcomed the draft law on special solutions supporting persons with significant disabilities and asks the High Sejm to approve the above. The bill's draft. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u242	Thank you, Member. The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 5 of the agenda: Report of the Administrative and Internal Affairs Committee on the Government's draft law on the processing of Passenger Name Record data (Prints No 2461 and 2481). I would very much like to ask Mr Bartosz Józwiak to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u243	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the report of the Board of Administration and Home Affairs on the Government's draft law on the processing of Passenger Name Record data, print No 2461. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs for first reading on 20 April 2018. The law in question lays down the rules and conditions for air carriers to transmit Passenger Name Record data and to process such data in order to detect and combat terrorist offences and other criminal offences or tax offences and to prevent and prosecute the perpetrators thereof, and identifies the persons responsible in these cases. The draft Passenger Name Record Processing Act aims to implement Directive (EU) 2016/681 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the use of Passenger Name Record (PNR) data for the purpose of preventing terrorist offences and, as I mentioned, others, including fiscal offences. The Directive lays down rules for air carriers to transmit Passenger Name Record Data, the processing of such data by Member States of the European Union and the exchange of such data between Member States in order to prevent such offences. For the purposes of the proposed act, PNR data are Passenger Name Record data, including personal data that are processed in connection with the conduct of business by air carriers for the purpose of booking or flight execution in the course of air transport, subject to transfer by the air carrier to the JIP. The above definition also refers to data already collected by air carriers in connection with their business activities. The categories of PNR data that can be processed on the basis of the proposed law are set out mainly in the article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1. This provision contains the PNR data catalogue entered by air carriers and specifies the data to be transmitted. It is a closed catalogue. The scope of the project shall take into account the purpose of the processing of data set out in the Directive, namely the prevention of identified offences. The draft law shall also specify the entities that will be competent in matters relating to the processing of PNR data. The wording used in the draft law for the relevant entities covers all entities that will be involved in the processing of PNR data and, therefore, in the scope of the project, account shall be taken of the fact that not only the competent authorities but also the National Passenger Information Unit, Europol, the competent authorities and passenger information units in other Member States as well as the authorities of third countries are authorised to process the data. It is worth pointing out that the rules in force today do not regulate matters concerning the processing of PNR data in order to combat crime. The current legal situation only regulates the transmission by air carriers, at the request of the competent Border Guard Service Commandant, of information on passengers on board the aircraft. The Act also contains a detailed list of conditions which must be met each time and comprehensively by countries wishing to receive such data or to have access to such data. Implementation of the provisions of the Directive in the Polish legal order requires, first of all, the obligation for air carriers to transfer PNR data. The Directive sets out the obligation for air carriers to provide these data which relate to non-EU passengers, but at the same time, in accordance with the Directive, a Member State may decide to apply the provisions of the Directive also to intra-EU flights, as is the case with Poland. Our law also decided to include intra-EU flights, as in most EU countries, in those which have already implemented this directive, i.e. in three countries, and those which are considering it for the time being. The proposed law also requires air carriers to inform each passenger about the collection and transfer of PNR data to the system and their right to protect their personal data. 163 legislative amendments were tabled during the committee's work. Several substantive amendments were made alongside this. In fact, most of these amendments were adopted, with the exception of one amendment clarifying one of the provisions concerning the conditions fulfilled by the EU countries which are to have access to this information. There was also a request for a subcommittee. This proposal has also not been accepted by a majority of committee members. The Commission adopted the Act in the absence of votes against and with three abstentions. In conclusion, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 7 May 2018, brings forward: The High Sejm will approve the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u244	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Ewa Filipiak, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u245	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the law and justice club, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the draft law on the processing of Passenger Name Record data. This is print number 2461. The draft Passenger Name Record Processing Act aims at transposing Directive 2016/681 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016. The Act sets out the terms and conditions for air carriers to transmit and process Passenger Name Records in order to prevent, detect and prosecute terrorist offences and other criminal offences or tax offences and to prosecute the perpetrators of such offences. The Directive sets out the obligation for air carriers to provide PNR data relating to non-EU passengers. At the same time, in accordance with the Directive, a Member State may decide to apply the provisions of the Directive also to intra-EU flights. The projector, our government has benefited from the possibility of applying the proposed law also to flights between Member States. The use of PNR data processing procedures also for intra-EU flights is necessary to ensure the security of the State. Terrorist threats are increasingly diagnosed within the European Union as a result of the movement between EU Member States of persons presenting a specific risk. PNR data collected on intra-EU flights have the same value in the context of countering terrorist threats and other crime as PNR data collected on non-EU flights. The draft law provides that air carriers will be required to transmit PNR data before each scheduled flight, as well as to provide for different situations related to the departure of passengers on board the aircraft. The Directive also requires that each Member State establish or designate a competent authority to act as a passenger information unit, so-called. JIP. The draft law provides that this role will be entrusted to the Border Guard. The Head of the Border Guard will be responsible for the establishment, operation and operation of the National PNR Information System. The proposed law also regulates the rules for the processing, storage, depersonalisation and deletion of PNR data collected by JIPs from air carriers. The draft provides for this in Article. Article 33 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 that PNR data which may be used for the direct identification of a passenger 6 months after their collection in KSI PNR will be depersonalised by making them invisible to the data processors. The proposed law also provides for the regulation of international cooperation in the exchange of PNR data. The Directive provides that PNR data collected by the JIP may be exchanged between Member States and transferred to Europol and third countries in order to prevent, detect and detect the offences referred to therein. The draft law proposes to regulate the rules and modalities for the transmission of these data separately for each of the above mentioned. entities. The provisions of the Act provide for administrative fines to be imposed on air carriers for non-fulfilment or incorrect fulfilment of obligations under the Act. The Act is expected to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. The Law and Justice Club supports the bill and recommends its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Special Services, the obligation to minimise the effects and causes of the most serious crimes, including crimes committed by persons, and to prevent them from leaving the place of residence, i.e. a country in which these persons have committed serious crimes against the vital interests of the country concerned, e.g. of a carnal nature. In general, the data received from carriers are to be deleted after the border inspection of passengers of the flight has been completed on the territory of the Republic of Poland, which must take place no later than 24 hours after the date of transmission of the information by the air carrier. The solutions in this respect constitute the transposition of Council Directive 2004/82/EC, which has already been implemented in Polish law by the Law of 3 July 2002. Within the meaning of the proposed act, the term "data' shall be understood to mean Passenger Name Record data, including personal data which are processed in connection with the conduct of business by air carriers for the purpose of making a reservation or the execution of a given flight. The proposed solution is also intended to facilitate international cooperation in the field of data exchange, which will be done by using similar concepts by authorities in other countries. The use in the draft law, as the Directive points out, of the concepts of serious crime will avoid the need to identify the appropriate legal bases for the specific offences provided for by the directive and will also include fiscal offences. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The matter discussed today is very delicate, complicated, and perhaps on many issues and controversial. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u258	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u259	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Free and Solidarni Circle regarding the government's draft law on the processing of Passenger Name Record data. In the 21st century, one of the biggest threats is terrorism, one of its manifestations is suicide attacks, terrorists constantly improve their methods, they learn security systems, so we should improve our mechanisms every once in a while. This draft law aims at transferring to Poland the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the use of Passenger Name Record Data for the prevention, detection, investigation and prosecution of terrorist offences and serious crime. The Directive clarifies the regulations relating to the collection and analysis of data and the transmission of data between the countries of the European Union in order to prevent, detect, investigate and prosecute terrorist offences and serious crime. In the current legal state it is the Border Guard that comes to the carrier to make available the list of passengers on board the aircraft, which will land on the territory of the Republic of Poland. Upon receipt of the request, the air carrier shall collect the required passenger data and forward them to the requesting authority. Data received from carriers shall be deleted 24 hours after receipt. The whole procedure can often take very valuable time. The new legal order will require air carriers to transmit data relating to non-EU passengers. At the same time, in accordance with the Directive, a Member State may decide to apply the provisions of the Directive also to intra-EU flights. According to Art. 2 (2) 1 and 2 Directives: If a Member State decides to apply this Directive to intra-EU flights, it shall inform the Commission thereof in writing. This bill sets out the entities that will process passenger data. It will be among others.: National Passenger Information Unit (JIP), Europol, competent authorities and passenger information units in other Member States, as well as third country authorities. According to Art. Article 6 (6) (a) l of the proposed law is intended to require air carriers to submit data before each scheduled flight within two time limits - between 48 and 24 hours prior to the scheduled start of the flight and immediately after the completion of the ticket and baggage check-in and the boarding of passengers on the aircraft when they can no longer leave before the flight takes off and other passengers cannot board. The Directive also requires that each Member State establish an authority to administer the data provided by carriers. In Poland this body will be the Border Guard. It is an individual who has the right competence for such activities and already has experience in ensuring the security of Polish borders. The adoption of adaptation provisions is a necessity for us in connection with the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the use of Passenger Name Record Data for the prevention, detection, investigation and prosecution of terrorist offences and serious crime. In addition, the new rules will have a positive impact on the detection of planned terrorist attacks and the elimination of crime. In recent years, terrorists have often traveled across Europe, making it difficult for the same services to control them. The introduction in Poland of provisions of a government bill on the processing of Passenger Name Record data will make it easier to catch potential terrorists. Thank you.

Its provisions do not include the authorisation of the administrative authority to receive statements from applicants for a driving licence under criminal liability for the provision of false data. Thus, the Supreme Court has ruled that it is impossible to convict for writing lies in a statement if the obligation to make a statement is not included in the bill. The proposed law sanctiones the existing arrangements set out in the Annex to the Regulation of the Minister for Infrastructure and Construction of 24 February 2016 on the issuing of documents stating the right to drive which required the submission of such declarations. After the amendments, the obligation for the applicant to make such a declaration will result directly from the provisions of the Act. Any false statement will threaten to be sanctioned. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u279	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the position of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle on the Commission's draft law amending the Act on Vehicle Managers. This Act is the consequence of a petition by one of the citizens who pointed out the urgent need to amend the rules so that the applicant and the holder of the licence to drive the tram threaten to have real legal consequences in the event of a false declaration. The current state of law does not allow the person making the false statement to be punished. In its judgment of 3 September 2015, the Supreme Court stated that the provisions relating to the issue of a driving licence do not authorise the administration to withdraw from persons who apply for a driving licence, statements under criminal liability for providing false data on the subject of a driving ban. As there is no such provision in the Act, it is not possible to convict for making a false statement, despite prejudice of criminal liability. This Act sanctiones the existing solution, as set out in the Annex to the Regulation of the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction of 24 February 2016 on the issuance of documents stating entitlement to drive. The new provisions of the Act will be replaced by the wording which will require the person in charge to make a statement that: "It has not been ordered against it by a final judgment of the court, a ban on driving, no driver's license or a licence to drive a tram, no right to drive vehicles has been revoked.' The changes made will benefit from the coherence and transparency of the legal system. Everyone will be able to be sure that a driving licence is issued only to persons who meet certain requirements and that motorists who have obtained a driving licence in breach of the regulations (they must have withheld information) will be held liable. In view of the above, the Free and Solidarni Circle supports the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u282	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Bożena Borys-Raccoon, Law and Justice will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u283	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present to the High House a position vis-à-vis the Government's draft law amending the Trade Union Act and certain other laws, 1933 and 2420 prints. The proposed amendments aim, in particular, at the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 2 June 2015 in the form of an application. Act K 1/13. In the statement of reasons for that judgment, the Court indicated that freedom of association in the trade unions referred to in Article. 59 (1) (a) 1 of the Constitution, any worker within the constitutional meaning, and an employee as an entity of freedom of association in trade unions cannot be identified solely by the nature of the legal relationship between him and his employer. In the current legal state, the right to create and join trade unions is not subject to such a wide range. It mainly serves employees within the meaning of the Labour Code. The international treaties treat freedom of association of persons in organisations set up to defend rights and represent professional, economic and social interests as fundamental human rights. Active people associate in trade unions to defend their interests. In order to achieve, maintain and multiply trade union rights and professional, economic, social and partly political interests, they organize protest actions - strikes. Therefore, in the Polish legal system, legal arrangements should be provided to ensure that freedom of association covers all employees, not only employees within the meaning of the Labour Code. This objective is fully implemented by the legal draft. The main purpose of the amendment is to enable the creation of trade unions and to join them, in particular, self-employed people and those who are based on civil law contracts, such as contract contracts, service contracts or contract for works. This is to be done by the proposed definition of the concept of a person doing a job, according to which a worker or a person providing a job for a remuneration on a different basis than the employment relationship if he does not employ other persons for that type of work, irrespective of the basis of the employment, and has such professional interests associated with the performance of work as a group-protected person. In particular, it is about highlighting three elements defining the legal framework for the concept of worker as the entity of constitutional freedom of association in a trade union. The draft law also provides for the introduction of appropriate safeguard mechanisms guaranteeing independence from employers, autonomy and freedom of action for trade unions. This is a direct consequence of extending the scope of the right to establish and join trade unions. It is difficult to imagine the effective performance by trade unions of their functions without giving them the appropriate powers. I would like to stress that the solutions proposed in this project have been developed with the cooperation of the social partners, particularly within the framework of the Problem Group on. Labour law of the Social Dialogue Council. In addition, representatives of both trade unions and employers' organisations participated in numerous meetings of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. In conclusion, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I find that the proposed law rightly extends the right to establish trade unions and to join them, especially those employed under civil law agreements. The non-workable categories of workers deserve to be strengthened also in collective labour law. I believe that remodeling the scope of the subjective law of the coalition will lead to many important social changes. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I ask you to continue to process the bill and to state that the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law presented by the government. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u301	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft is contained in Seym printing No 2391 and the committee's report is in print No 2486. Ladies and gentlemen! Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 6 April 2018, the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs for first reading. The reason for the amendment of the Act on Occupational Pensions is the need to adapt the provisions of the current Act on Occupational Pensions of 20 April 2004 to the provisions of Directive 2014/50 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on minimum requirements to increase the mobility of workers between Member States by facilitating the acquisition and maintenance of supplementary pension rights. The most important changes proposed in the draft concern, among others. determining the maximum waiting period for the acquisition of the right to join the supplementary pension scheme. The draft provides for the introduction into the article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1c entry with the following wording: The establishment contract may not provide for more than 3 years of traineeship entitlement to participate in the programme. The next amendment concerns the extension of the information obligations of employers with occupational pension schemes vis-à-vis programme participants, former employees and persons entitled to receive the money they raise in the event of the death of the programme participant. In addition, managers of occupational pension schemes were required to immediately inform employers running the programme, at their request, of the value of the funds collected in the employee's account - an active participant - or former employee. In addition, according to the project, the supervisory authority was required by 31 January 2019 to amend the register of occupational pension schemes for employers who did not comply with the obligation to notify the adaptation of works contracts to the proposed provisions. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed solutions do not affect the competitiveness of the economy and entrepreneurship, including micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. These solutions should not create burdens for employers, as they are already obliged to provide workers with the scope of information set out in the Act on occupational pension schemes. The amended law is to enter into force on the day following the date of publication in the Official Journal, as on 21 May 2018 the deadline for implementing Directive 2014/50 of the European Parliament and of the Council expires. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Social Policy and Family Committee, following its first reading and consideration at its meeting on 7 May 2018, requests the adoption by the Sejm of a draft law in the form of print No 2391. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u303	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Employee Pension Programmes, print no. 2391. High Seym! The draft amendment of the law adapts Polish law to the EU directive, which aims to facilitate the exercise of the right to free movement of workers between the countries of the European Union. One of the main areas to be amended is the definition of the maximum waiting period for the employee to acquire the right to join the supplementary pension scheme. The current regulations leave freedom to the parties to the works contract in this respect, providing only for minimum traineeships which should not be less than 3 months. According to the draft article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1c of the Act the acquisition of the entitlement to join the occupational pension scheme will require a maximum of 3 years of employment. From an employee's perspective, this means that in order to join the programme, he will have to register with the employer for 3 months to 3 years, depending on what the establishment contract provides for in this respect. In accordance with the provisions of the Article. 2 The Act amending the Works Contract providing for more than 3 years of service entitlement to participate in the Staff Pension Scheme on the day preceding the date of entry into force of the new regulations should be adapted to the new provisions by 31 December 2018. The amendment of the Act on occupational pension schemes also provides for the addition of two new information obligations for current and former participants in occupational pension schemes or persons entitled to receive funds collected by them. Thus, in relation to the current PPE participants, the employer will be obliged to provide the participant with clear information on how the termination of the participant's employment will affect its powers to the funds collected under the PPE. On the other hand, information obligations for former PPE participants will also include information requirements for information not more than once a year on the value of the funds collected in the account of the former EPP participant and on the conditions for their treatment. The same scope of information will have to be communicated by the employer to the persons entitled to receive the funds collected by the participant in the event of his death. In addition, managers of occupational pension schemes were required to promptly provide employers running the programme with information on the value of the funds collected in the employee's account, an active participant, or a former employee at their request. The solution contained in the project should not create a burden on employers, as they already have to provide workers with the information set out in the Act on Occupational Pensions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed changes are beneficial for workers - participants in occupational pension schemes. In view of the positive effects of the proposed amendments, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Government's draft law amending the Act on occupational retirement schemes. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u305	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform is pleased to present its position on the Act amending the Act on Employee Pension Programmes in print no. 2391, following the meeting of the Social Policy and Family Committee held on 7 May 2018. The purpose of this law is to implement the provisions of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014. The Directive lays down provisions to facilitate the exercise of the right to free movement of workers between Member States of the European Union by reducing obstacles arising from certain rules on supplementary pension schemes related to employment relations. The existing provisions set out in the current Act provide for a minimum period of employment entitled to participate in the occupational pension scheme. They shall also allow the freedom specified in the works contract. On the other hand, the draft law provides that an undertaking contract may not provide for more than 3 years of employment with the employer in question, which entitles him to participate in occupational pension schemes. This draft law also introduces an information obligation for employers, covering the scope of the individual information of the participant in the pension scheme concerning the conditions for the acquisition of rights, the value of the rights acquired, the conditions for the future treatment of inactive rights and instructions in the event of reimbursement. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendments made are of an adaptation and technical nature. They regulate existing solutions and introduce some not too burdensome obligations for employers. Parliamentary Club The Civic Platform will vote in favour of the proposed amendment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u309	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Employee Retirement Programmes, print no. 2391. The purpose of the amendment is to implement Directive 2014/50/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on minimum requirements to increase the mobility of workers between Member States by facilitating the acquisition and maintenance of supplementary pension rights. The amendment of the PPE Act raises three issues: it sets out the maximum permissible waiting period for one employer to acquire entitlement to the supplementary pension scheme for up to 3 years, as until now the Act did not specify the maximum waiting period for the traineeship, so that employers could determine this time at their discretion or not at all; extending the information obligations of employers operating occupational pension schemes to their members, former employees and persons entitled to receive funds collected by the participants of the scheme in the event of their death; and regulates the transmission of information by PPE managers to the employer. Positive aspects of the project. The amendments aim to facilitate the acquisition of PPE information in the event of a cessation of employment relationship with the employer concerned, in order to facilitate the decision-making of the form of continuation of PPE in the form of the new employer, if the latter leads the PPE, or the IKE. Guaranteeing a worker maximum waiting time for the right to join the occupational pension scheme - a period of 3 years of work seems optimal in today's labour market conditions. It is, as I understand it, to increase the availability of PPE for a wider range of employees of a given workplace and to increase the equality of employees of the employer over 3 years of work.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u310	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Of course, such a program has its drawbacks, among others. setting as low as possible contributions, but also brings many positives. These solutions vary from country to country in the European Union in terms of the rules on participation, the universality of the system or the way in which contributions are financed. The programmes have mainly spread where they are mandatory or quasi-mandatory. Tax benefits are also an effective way to encourage people to participate. The Polish project draws much from British patterns, but the authors of the Polish solution tried to avoid its weaknesses. In the Polish system, the contract will have the character of an automatic contract. Employers who do not choose the provider of the pension plan will automatically be assigned to the TFI PFR. The British solution is based on tax exemption, which is preferred by wealthy people and what is costly for you. In Poland, there is to be an additional amount. Such a solution is more favourable to less earners. Employee pension scheme managers were required to report immediately to programme employers, upon their request, information on the value of the funds collected in the employee's (active participant's) or former employee's account, as employers are not always in possession of the required set of information. According to the project, the supervisory authority was required by 31 January 2019 to amend the register of occupational pension schemes for employers who did not comply with the obligation to notify the adaptation of works contracts to the wording of the proposed law.
