ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u312	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on National Defence, I have the honour to present a report on the government's draft amendment to the Act on the Signs of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, prints no. 2387 and 2444. The first reading of the draft law took place at committee meetings on 11 and 12 April. Following a discussion at both meetings of the committee, the draft was adopted in its original form without any amendments. What does the project involve? The core of the project is the introduction of territorial defence symbols, which belong to the types of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The territorial defence forces receive a military eagle and a flag. The Eagle of the Territorial Defence Army, as well as the sign used by the Land Army, will be an eagle that sits on the shield of the Amazon, in which it is inscribed the Sign of Poland Fighting. The same military eagle will also be on the flag, which will be dedicated to the Territorial Defence Army. High Seym! The discussion, which took place at the meeting of the National Defence Committee, focused mainly on the validity of the use of the Sign of Poland Fighting as a sign which points to the specificity of the Territorial Defence Forces. Members of the opposition raised doubts in this regard. We would like to quote here the arguments which were included in the justification of the law, as well as the arguments which Members raised during the meeting of the Committee on National Defence, which indicated that the Mark of Poland Fighting is a symbol which refers to the proud tradition of fighting and resistance of the Polish people to the German occupation, as well as to the Soviet occupation during World War II. It refers to the heritage of the Polish Underground State and its army, the National Army. This heritage is one of the most important elements not only of our independence tradition, but also of our national identity. By using the Sign of Poland Fighting as a sign characterizing this type of Armed Forces, i.e. the Army of Territorial Defence, it thus refers directly to the heritage of the Polish Underground State and its army. In this way, the Territorial Defence Forces symbolically also become a continuation of this tradition: the tradition of the Polish Underground State and the tradition of the National Army. Is this legitimate? Definitely. First of all, the National Army was a common territorial military organization. Its command structures and training centers, as well as the troops of areas, districts, subregions, circuits and facilities included the whole state, the municipalities and villages included. The newly created Territorial Defence Forces are also a territorial armed formation whose primary task will be to defend on-site. Secondly, the establishment of the Territorial Defence Army is the culmination of the long-term efforts made by the Polish Army's community as well. It is worth recalling here, for example, a letter from the President of the World Association of National Army Soldiers of 7 December 2000 to the then Minister of National Defence, which was then none other than Bronisław Komorowski. At that time, the President of the World Union of National Army Soldiers addressed the Minister of National Defence, expressed in this letter the desire that the territorial structures of territorial defence should adopt and continue the traditions of the AK-voy. At the same time, he stressed that the continuation of the tradition of the Home Army by territorial defence would be a great tribute both to the fight and the victims of the Home Army soldiers, as well as to the use of invaluable organisational and military experience of the Home Army in creating an effective defence force of the Republic of Poland. Unfortunately, it took a while for the Home Army soldiers to carry out this will, but it was worth it. This was possible at the time of the formation of the Territorial Defence Army as a separate type of Armed Forces. Thanks to this 28th of June Ub. r. The World Union of National Army Soldiers has passed on the traditions of the General Command of the National Army of Territorial Defence. High Izbo! This heritage, the heritage of the Polish Underground State and its Home Army, has been passed on to us by the past generation. The current generation should continue this legacy. The acquisition by the Army of Territorial Defence of the symbolism of the Polish Underground State and its Home Army is the best example of this and the best symbol. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on National Defence, I recommend the adoption of a draft law which amends the law on the signs of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u314	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Act on the Signs of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. The amendment of the law proposed by the government provides for the introduction for the Army of Territorial Defence of the symbolism of the type of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland: the military eagle and the flag of the type of Armed Forces. The Territorial Defence Army as a new type of Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland are a very important element of strengthening the military capacity of our country. We have very positive experience in forming the first three forces of the Territorial Defence Army in Eastern Poland. Okay. 7,000. volunteers who have already taken a soldier's oath in the Army of Territorial Defence, are people of different ages, different professions, people with excellent competences useful in modern army, but above all they are patriots ready to sacrifice for our homeland. As patrons of the brigades and battalions, they take heroes, commanders of the Home Army, leaders of the Polish Underground State and enchanted by Communists soldiers of integrity. The proposed law is in a sense a response to this bottom-up patriotic attitude of the soldiers of the Territorial Defence Army. Currently, in the Land Army, inheritors of the oldest sign, shaped still in 1815, is used an eagle sitting on the shield of amazons. Signs of other types of Armed Forces were created on the basis of adding to this historical pattern elements indicating their tradition and specificity. And so: in the Navy the shield field is dark blue, and on the eagle a golden anchor of the admirality was placed, in the Air Force on the sides of the eagle are placed Huss wings based on the shield of amazons, in the Special Army the shield of amazons is black. The same principle was also adopted in the case of the pattern of the Eagle of the Territorial Defence Army, imposing on the shield amazon gold sign of Poland Fighting. These are flags. The use in the image of the eagle and the flag of the Sign of Poland Fighting emphasizes the fact that defending the homeland is a matter and a duty of all citizens of the Republic. This symbol of the fight between the Polish people and the Nazi invader refers to the tradition to which contemporary soldiers of the Territorial Defence Army refer, i.e. the heroes of the Polish Underground State and the Home Army, as well as the cursed soldiers. The design of the eagle and flags is a simple, yet aesthetic and readable symbol for all the Territorial Defence Army. At the same time, with the in-service arrangements, a public consultation was carried out with the Kombatant organizations associating soldiers of the National Army and insurgents of Warsaw and with associations dealing with state and military symbolism. According to the consultations conducted with the Kompatan environments, these environments supported the project. They only submitted certain technical comments, which were taken into account and accepted in the project. The draft was also addressed to the Heraldic Commission for an opinion. This committee did not initially deliver an opinion within the time limit set. Following the adoption of the bill in November 2017 by the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers, the Heraldic Commission finally expressed its opinion - it was a negative opinion - stating that the use of the symbol of Poland Fighting in the Eagle of the Territorial Defence Army does not indicate the specificity of the Territorial Defence Army. This opinion is not shared by the drafter of the law, the majority of the Members of the Committee on National Defence, who have consulted the draft, nor by the Members of my club, the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, on whose behalf I have the honour of presenting this position. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of this law. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u324	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Although this is a small amendment, it concerns a matter of great importance for many people, both combatants fighting under the Sign of Poland Fighting for an independent homeland, their families and descendants of those who died in this fight, as well as citizens serving today in the newly established Territorial Defence Forces. This law gives the symbol of Poland Fighting Territorial Defence, a symbol of the hope of regaining independence and freedom, a symbol for which thousands of Poles gave their lives, i.e. the holy symbol. While the use of the image of an eagle sitting on a shield of amazons accompanying a Polish soldier since 1815 is obvious, the appearance on the shield of the Sign of Poland Fighting raises legitimate controversy. In the justification of the project we can read that the use in the image of the Eagle and the flag of the Sign of Poland Fighting emphasizes the fact that defending the homeland is a matter and a duty of all citizens of the Republic of Poland. True, defending the homeland is the duty of all citizens, but how does the Sign of the Fighting Poland say about it? During World War II, this symbol was intended to comfort the people of the occupied state, to show that among ordinary citizens who were not actively fighting the invaders, there was a resistance movement seeking to regain independence. Like I said, it was a symbol of hope and heroes fighting for independence. Then, in the justification of the project, we come across information about the public consultations conducted with the Kombatant organizations associating soldiers of the National Army and Warsaw insurgents and associations dealing with state and military symbolism. As we learn from the further content, the combatant environments, which have passed on their traditions to the Territorial Defence Army, supported the project. At this point, it is worth looking at the manipulation and wicked behavior that the designers have committed towards the veterans of World War II. When on 28 June 2017, the former Minister of National Defence announced the takeover by WOT Command of the tradition of the Home Army General Command, Michał Dworczyk, then State Secretary at the Ministry of National Defence, informed that the authorities of the World Association of National Army Soldiers accepted the use of the Polish Sign Fighting by the Territorial Defence Army. This is true, although according to the information provided by Mr Tadeusz Filipkowski's spokesperson, this decision was accompanied by lively discussions among veterans. However, in the justification of the project there is also a discussion with the Kombatant organizations which bring together Warsaw insurgents and associations dealing with state and military symbolism. This is where I mentioned earlier the wickedness and manipulation of the designer. On 28 June 2017, the Warsaw Uprising Union had no information about the acquisition of the symbol of Poland Fighting planned by the Ministry of Defence. It was only 2 days later that the union received a letter asking for an opinion on the proposal to amend the law on the signs of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. Moreover, the document includes a request for urgent consultations and an instruction that if the Warsaw Insurgents Union fails to provide an answer to the Ministry by 7 July, it will consider that the insurgents have accepted the project. In the opinion of the Poselski Club, such treatment of the heroes of the Warsaw Uprising is unacceptable. We believe that top-down recognition of the lack of an answer, whose deadline was grossly short, for accepting the project is not an abuse, but an expression of total arrogance and disrespect for the fallen and fighting for independence of Poland's heroes. The urgency applied in the case of a law which does not require rapid proceding is without object. These are, of course, procedural issues, but in the case of an ideological law relating to higher values, procedural issues, the way in which the law was adopted, whether there has been extensive consultation with all the parties directly involved without unnecessary sharing of these environments, are becoming tangible. Perhaps the situation was a prostration of the resentment of the former Minister of National Defence and the leadership of the PiS towards the Warsaw Insurgents' Union resulting from opposition to the Smolensk appeal. I was unable to reach the position of the last of the groups consulted in the explanatory memorandum, i.e. to associations dealing with national and military symbolism, but at this point I would like to recall the opinion of the Heraldic Commission. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed on 20 April the above draft law for first reading in the Rules of Procedure Committee, Parliamentary Affairs and Immunity. The first reading of the committee took place on 30 April. The draft envisages a revision of the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2th Act, giving it the wording, which shows that the salary of a the remuneration of the Undersecretary of State established on the basis of the provisions on the remuneration of managers of state positions, excluding the allowance for service of years, which consequently means a 20% reduction in the abovementioned remuneration. At the same time, the project assumes that financial resources will remain unchanged to cover expenditure incurred in the implementation of the mandate in the country and will amount to 25% the remuneration of the Under-Secretary of State, established on the basis of the rules on remuneration of managers of state positions, excluding the allowance for service of years. The draft law is linked to social expectations regarding the salaries of Members and Senators. [[Voice from the hall: With prizes.]] The project has a positive social and financial impact. The revenue of the state budget for reducing parliamentary and senatorial salaries... [[Voice from the room: Just don't steal!]] ...will increase annually by about PLN 13 million. The project assumed that the Act would enter into force on the first day of the month following the announcement. However, there is a danger that such a short term could pose a problem in calculating the salaries of Members and Senators if it were only a few days. The Commission therefore accepted that the Act would enter into force on the first day of the second month following the month of publication. [[Voice from the room: So you saved yourself another salary.]] The Commission, after first reading and considering this proposal at its meeting on 30 April, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u418	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm on 25 April 2018 referred the draft law amending the Law on the amendment of the Law on social security benefits in the event of sickness and maternity and certain other laws to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. The Commission, at its meeting yesterday, at first reading, considered the above. Parliamentary draft law from print No. 2463. I would like to remind you that the Act of 25 June 1999 on social security benefits in the case of sickness and maternity governs the rules for granting sickness benefits and the method for calculating them and lays down rules for documenting the right to these benefits, while the Act of 15 May 2015 amends the abovementioned. the law and introduces changes in the form and mode of issuing temporary incapacity certificates. In addition to the existing form of medical certification, so-called. on paper, on printing ZUS ZLA it introduces a new electronic form, e-ZLA, which goes directly to the Social Insurance Institution from 1 January 2016. According to the Act for the envisaged transitional period until 30 June this year. both forms of temporary incapacity certificates may be used: paper and electronic. By listening to the demands of the medical community, as well as by conducting analyses on the functioning of the solutions, especially taking into account all the voices, we believe that more time should be given to implement new solutions to enable all users of the electronic system, in particular doctors, to prepare for this change. It will certainly be an additional time to implement solutions allowing medical staff to issue medical sick leave on behalf of their physician, which will allow doctors to focus on treatment and diagnosis. It is therefore proposed to amend the Act of 15 May 2015 to extend the transitional period, so-called. two-track issue of medical certificates on temporary incapacity for work, until 30 November 2018 In the course of the work in committee, the Legislative Bureau notified a change concerning the first part of the title of the project. The Act amending the Act was cited as amended by the Act amending the Act, etc. As a last resort, the entire title of the proposed law is: the Act amending the Law on the amendment of the Law on social security benefits in the case of sickness and maternity and certain other laws. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy has adopted the proposed amendment of the law unanimously and, after first reading and considering the project on 7 May, brings forward: The High Sejm will approve the attached draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u419	Thank you very much, Member. ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u420	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present an opinion on the draft law amending the Law on the amendment of the Law on social security benefits in the case of sickness and maternity and certain other laws contained in print No 2463. The introduction of the possibility of issuing medical certificates in electronic form improves the efficiency of the handling process, significantly reduces the time needed to complete the medical certificate, increases the ability to carry out checks, automatically informs the payers of the certificates issued to the insured, but also, which is very important, limits the possibility of fraud. There is no turning back from computerization in health care, but preparation for this change should be given more time to all users of the new e-ZLA system. The planned changes may be introduced, among others. the possibility for doctors to issue electronic redundancies, e.g. Assistants. The draft law is not covered by European Union law, it does not impose a financial burden on either the State or local government units. It should be noted that the proposed regulation has a positive social impact. Taking into account the demands, the proposed amendment to the Law on the amendment of the Law on social security benefits in the case of sickness and maternity and certain other laws, consisting of extending the period of two-track medical certificates, is most appropriate. High Izbo! In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposed bill with the proposed amendment to the title. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u422	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Club, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Law on the amendment of the Law on social security benefits in the event of illness and maternity and certain other laws, print No 2463. The amendment of the Act concerns the Act of 15 May 2015, in which the printing of ZUS ZLA - a new electronic form of medical certificate on temporary incapacity to work due to illness, hospital stay or the need to take care of a sick family member - was introduced alongside the existing form of medical certificate. The Act provides for a transitional period during which both forms of certificate may be used. This period is from 1 January 2016 to 30 June 2018. Only the electronic form of the certificate shall apply after this period. The amendment we are talking about extends the transitional period until 30 November 2018. Everything would be fine, except there were 2 years to put in place this system, while the information we received at the committee's meeting shows that only 1/4 of the medical offices are currently using electronic form for 2 years, ladies and gentlemen. You've set up four months to make 75%. Medical offices have adapted to this requirement. At this rate, there's a fear that you won't make it. I also did not get an answer to the question I asked during the committee meeting, whether you examined how the employers were prepared. In what percentage of employers who employ less than 5 people and are not obliged to register in the electronic certification system are prepared to introduce this system? ParlaMint-PL_2018-05-08-sejm-62-1.u435	Dear Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a draft law on Central Communication Port prepared by the government, which will enable not only the creation of the Central Communication Port, i.e. a multimodal transport hub, in the heart of Poland, but also the extension of the railway network, to remodel the transport system of the Republic of Poland. We are facing the possibility of creating the largest infrastructure project in the last 30 years. I am counting on support here not only in the context of the bill being prepared, but also in the further stages of the creation of the Central Communication Port, because I deeply believe that the idea of creating the best connected place not only in Poland, but also in our part Europe is deeply in the interests of each of the people of our country regardless of the political sympathies and views presented. Air traffic in the Republic of Poland is constantly growing and since 2010 we are aware that the largest Polish airport, the port of Chopin, will not be able to handle the increasing traffic. However, the construction of the Central Communication Port is not only the construction of the airport, it is also the construction of more than 800 km of new railway routes, it is the creation of an opportunity to reach the port of Solidarity in the time of 2,2,5 hours from the largest cities of Poland. The scale of this project prevents it from being effectively prepared and implemented on the basis of the existing legal order. Therefore, when preparing the bill, we have focused on ensuring the effectiveness of this investment and effective public control of the processes that will consist of it. Section I of the Act sets out the general principles on which the investment process is based. We are aware that the implementation of this project will not take place without problems. This investment is also unprecedented due to its scale. At the turn of May and June we will know the exact results of the localization analysis, which will help us to know the area for construction. From this point on, intensive consultations will begin with residents to develop compromise solutions on the challenge of acquiring land for this investment. We predict that 90% The areas needed for the establishment of the Central Communication Port will be purchased by way of purchase. The negotiations that will start this year will continue until the end of 2019, i.e. over 1.5 years. The Republic will endeavour to ensure that the agreements are as beneficial as possible for the local community. Since we wrote this law in such a way as to get as far away from local communities as possible because of their concerns, although the investment is carried out in the interests of all inhabitants of the Republic of Poland, it must be conducted with respect for the interests of the local community. Section II of the Act is the body part. In the systemic part, the powers of the proxy are extended to the existing regulation and have also been established, the powers of the special purpose company, which will be established under the law, have been specified. Thus, it will be up to the proxy to supervise the strategic assumptions of the project, to prepare draft government strategy papers, to approve management rules, which will bring together several hundred investments in one whole. Finally, the draft provides that the Minister responsible for Transport will carry out his duties with regard to the aviation sector by means of the Secretary of State or Undersecretary of State who is the representative of the Government for the Central Communication Port. On the other hand, the special purpose company established under this Act will be involved in the operational management of this programme. In other words, the company will prepare the programme, ensure that the timetable is respected, manage the real estate generated for this investment, bear the costs of managing these properties and prepare a draft management rules that will shape the method by which the investment will be created. Governance rules are a certain novelty introduced by this law, an element that allows for the coordination of hundreds of projects that will consist of a programme for the construction of the Central Communication Port. Section III of the Act regulates the preparation and implementation of investments. The stage of investment preparation begins with the adoption of a regulation by the Council of Ministers on the basis of which the maximum area covered by environmental research and the maximum area of potential impact of the Act will be designated. This area will be covered by the municipalities and areas necessary for the preparation and implementation of the Central Communication Port. The next stage will be the reservation of the area, which will take place by means of a local act issued by the voivode. In this respect, there will be an extension of the powers of public authorities, an investment freeze. The aim is to ensure that the value of the areas to be covered by the future investment is not unduly increased. The Act also provides for the creation of an objective tool by which we can negotiate with property owners. These are property market expertise prepared by property experts. The aim is to ensure transparency and fair balance of the general interest with the interests of the inhabitants. We will take care of this, and I would like to stress this once again, so that the inhabitants of the areas where the Central Communication Port will be established have the opportunity - if they wish - to continue farming. They will not only obtain a favourable offer, a favourable price, if they are interested in monetary gratification, but will also obtain replacement properties on which, if they wish, they will be able to continue their agricultural activities. A specific feature of the Act is the separation of investments which are defined in the Act as accompanying investments. These are investments which are isolated due to the essential influence and essential importance of their implementation for the implementation of the entire programme, because - never enough repetition - the construction of the Central Communication Port is not only the construction of an intermodal junction in the middle of Poland, but also the construction, reconstruction and remodeling of the collective transport system in the Republic of Poland. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am convinced that it is possible to cooperate in the implementation of this project, which is why I ask for its positive assessment and acceptance in the form proposed by the government. Thank you.

Unfortunately, now by the PiS government money for Poland will be less. The government's dispute with Brussels regarding the politicisation of courts, the Constitutional Tribunal or the clipping of the Białowieża Forest remains important. In recent days, the EU Commissioners have presented a draft budget for the European Union in which Central Europe, including Poland, is receiving less funding. Greater money is to be given to those countries that have greater unemployment and are taking part in the fight against the effects of illegal immigration - Greece, Italy and Spain. Poland, as the largest beneficiary of European funds, can lose the most. In addition, the European Commission is working on changes in the link between the rule of law and the budget of the European Union. In the new budgetary perspective, a mechanism may be put in place to allow the suspension of payments to countries whose governments do not comply with the rule of law. What awaits us under the current rule? The allocation of EU funds is to be decided by a majority of the European Parliament and the European Commission. The mutual veto of Kaczyński and Orban is not enough to block this decision. Once again, it turns out that the actions of the PiS government can have a negative impact on investments carried out in Poland by means of European means, i.e. on the economic development of our country and finally on the resources of our portfolios.
